Nevada Cannabis & Hemp Research — AUDITED, Parts 0–12

Last audited: 31 October 2026. This file contains (1) the audit table, (2) everything still unverified, and (3) the complete rewritten report with every correction applied in place. The original research was dated 31 October 2026; the audit was performed against primary Nevada sources only, as commissioned. The federal baseline in the commission was used as given and was not re-audited, except to check that the report states it correctly.

Federal-baseline check — confirmed with one qualification. The report correctly states split scheduling (state-licensed medical and FDA-approved products Schedule III from 28 April 2026, 91 FR 22714; all adult-use Schedule I; the 2024 proposal not final), 280E relief for medical activity only, the hemp staging (PL 119-37 §781 effective 12 November 2026, limited by PL 119-103 §2019 until 11 December 2026, full total-THC definition and 0.4 mg per container cap after 11 December 2026), the USDA DEA-lab enforcement delay to 31 December 2026, the medical-marijuana rider through 11 December 2026, and no federal cannabis banking law. Qualification: do not state that the medical slice of a deemed dual licensee is Schedule III as settled law. CCB's 20 May 2026 Licensee Update says NRS 678D.490 deeming "presents a substantial and legitimate legal basis" for qualification, but "the Board cannot presently represent that the DEA has adopted, or will adopt" that reading; establishments holding a separate medical licence under NRS 678B.215 "appear to present the clearest case". Dual licensees must split medical and adult-use activity in Metrc and point-of-sale. Every consumption-lounge sale is adult-use only, so lounge sales remain Schedule I and fully subject to 280E on either reading. Tribal medical activity under a state compact counting as "state-licensed" is Verify — no federal determination was found, and CCB split-payment guidance says tribal establishments are not CCB-licensed, which is not itself a federal determination. DC Circuit stay status as of 31 October 2026 is Verify (June 2026 minutes establish only that a stay motion had been filed).

Part 1 — Audit table

Verdicts: Confirmed = primary source agrees; Corrected = wording/figure changed to the primary source; Removed = wrong and no correct version may be stated on this record; Unverified = only a secondary/mirror source exists, or the primary text was not opened. Secondary-only ballot percentages, prices, market shares and poll figures are never upgraded to official figures in this audit.

§1.6 / §7.1Nevada measures hemp THC on a total-isomer basis (delta-8 + delta-7 + delta-10 summed)CorrectedNAC 557.011, 557.042, 557.136Crop testing converts THCA→THC by post-decarboxylation (total potential delta-9); NAC 557.011 defines the acceptable level by reference to the delta-9 concentration in 7 U.S.C. §1639o. NRS 453.139's isomer-inclusive "THC" definition still matters for cannabis/hemp classification and NRS 439.532, but finished-product hemp THC is not stated here as a total-isomer sum. Finished-product THCA treatment outside crop testing: Verify.
§1.6 / §7.2NRS 557.255 synthetic-cannabinoid prohibition — wording not reachedCorrectedNRS 557.255Confirmed, scope narrowed: "A grower or producer shall not produce, distribute, sell in or offer to sell in this State any synthetic cannabinoid" (557.255(1), added 2021; "synthetic cannabinoid" as defined in NRS 678A.239). Growers/producers only — do not extend to all retail sellers in wording.
§1.6 / §7.7Human-consumption hemp/CBD testing/labelling under NRS 439.532; NAC 439.668–670 citedCorrectedNRS 439.532Substance confirmed: independent-lab testing + DHHS labelling; threshold is "the maximum THC concentration established by federal law for hemp"; "THC" cross-refers to NRS 453.139. Section numbers 439.668–670 not verified — cite R035-22 / NAC 439 generally until opened.

§2.8 / §3.1Local cannabis licence tax capped at 3% of gross revenue (NRS 244.35253 counties; 268.0977 cities); county may not impose it inside an incorporated cityConfirmedNRS 244.35253No change, with scope note: the 3% cap covers the licence tax under subsection (2); separate Ch. 278 fees and licence taxes on non-Chapter 678B activities (capped at the rate for similar businesses) sit outside it. Do not state "no local charge above 3%" without qualification.
§2.9Nevada §280E decoupling is "largely moot because Nevada has no personal income tax" (Art. 10, §1(9))CorrectedNev. Const. Art. 10, §1(9)Qualified: Art. 10, §1(9) bars tax on wages/personal income of natural persons but expressly permits taxes on business income/revenue. There is no Nevada personal/corporate income-tax return on which 280E disallowance would operate, so state conformity is moot in that sense — but Commerce Tax (gross-revenue) and Modified Business Tax are not deduction-based income taxes. Federal 280E consequences (medical-only relief, adult-use fully subject) are unaffected.
§2.10–2.11No hemp-specific excise tax (sales tax only); SB 281 (2021) 1% hemp excise failed; SB 41 (2025) Ch. 138, Cannabis Tax Permit effective 1 Jan 2027, existing sellers to apply by 1 Sep 2026; AB 307 (2025) wholesale-elimination / 14.25% retail failed; interim subcommittee recommended no specific changeConfirmedCAC Hemp Report, 10 Sep 2026; Dept. of Taxation Cannabis Tax Permit FAQsNo change to statuses, except: SB 41 consequence softened — CCB suspends the licence until compliance is cured (FAQ formulation), not "must revoke". AB 307 last action 3 June 2025, "No further action taken" — do not carry it as pending into 2027; any revival is a new bill number. SB 41 "1 July 2027 full-effect" secondary date conflicts with 1 Jan 2027 — do not resolve without NELIS.
§1.10AB 76 (2025) Ch. 459; AB 504 (2025) Ch. 484, eff. 1 Oct 2025; SB 157 (2025) eff. 1 Oct 2025; SB 168 (2025) enacted; SB 356 (2025) died 12 Apr 2025; AB 203, AB 149, AB 378 failed 3 June 2025; no regular session in 2026 (biennial, Art. 4, §2)ConfirmedSB 356 (NELIS)No change, except SB 25 (2025) chapter/effective date and AB 341 chapter number were not confirmed — keep Verify for those two. No 2025 statute limits intoxicating hemp to CCB licensees; SB 356 was that vehicle and it died — this correction to secondary coverage holds. Nevada implementation of the federal 12 Nov / 11 Dec 2026 hemp dates: Unavailable in opened official sources.
§3.1–3.2Local governments may ban adult-use establishments outright (zoning, 678D.510(1)(d)); state buffers are a floor; BUT localities "may not be more restrictive than state law on certain subjects"CorrectedNRS 678D.510; NRS 244.35253(5)Apparent contradiction resolved: the "more restrictive" ban is limited to (a) packaging/labelling/testing/dosage/potency, (b) kinds of products authorised under Title 56, (c) pesticides, (d) seed-to-sale tracking, (e) transport (other than direct-to-consumer delivery, plus transport notice). It does not bar zoning bans, stricter buffers, caps or lounge opt-outs. State buffers confirmed: airport property prohibited; 1,000 ft schools (preschool–grade 12, existing on application date); 300 ft community facilities; 1,500 ft nonrestricted gaming in counties ≥100,000 — measurement is from the front door to the closest point of the property line (678B.250(7)) — Verify flag removed.
§3.2–3.3Adult-use retail caps 80 / 20 / 4 / 2 by county population; lounge opt-out list 14 Oct 2022 (Boulder City, Carson City, Elko, Fernley, Henderson, Mesquite, North Las Vegas, Sparks, Yerington, Douglas, Eureka, Lander, Lincoln, Lyon, Mineral, Pershing, Winnemucca); Strip/Paradise/Spring Valley/Enterprise/Winchester = unincorporated Clark County, not City of Las Vegas; Carson City = consolidated municipalityConfirmedNRS 678B.260; CCB opt-out list, 14 Oct 2022Cap numbers confirmed — Verify flag removed, with caveat: post-SB 277, a deemed dual licence counts under medical limitations (678B.220/260/270) for cap purposes; reallocation to other counties where no applicant qualifies (678B.260(2)) applies. Opt-out is lounges only, not an establishment ban (e.g. Sparks, Henderson, North Las Vegas allow establishments while opted out of lounges). Same-name traps correctly avoided.

Douglas County all-districts prohibition is consistent with its code history. Lyon County status and Eureka County's 18 June 2026 vote: keep Verify (no current code/minutes text opened; do not upgrade Lyon draft/strike-through ordinances to adopted law). Lounge opt-out list currency since 14 Oct 2022: Verify.

§4.0, §4.36 CCB types, 7 in practice (lounges split); delivery is not a separate licence; agent cards are not establishment licences; SB 277 dual deeming; no new/renewed medical licence from 1 Jan 2024 except in a "covered jurisdiction" (prohibiting adult-use); medical-to-adult-use conversion "completed 31 Dec 2024"; adult-use retail caps; medical dispensary caps "40 / 10 / lower tiers Verify"CorrectedNRS 678B.215, 678B.220, 678B.260, 678D.490Mostly confirmed. Two corrections: (a) medical caps resolved — 55,000–<100,000: 2; each other county: 1; plus 1 per incorporated city in a county <100,000, subject to the 1-dispensary-per-10-pharmacies ratio in 678B.220(2) (add the ratio/city rules; delete the tier Verify flag). (b) "Conversion completed 31 Dec 2024" stays Verify — the 14 Feb 2025 CCB notice was not opened. Retail cap is for retail stores only, not all licence types; no statewide numeric cap on cultivation/production/distributor/lab in 678B.260 (absence there alone does not prove no cap exists in regulation).
§4.7Independent lounge: 20-licence cap, "whether it still binds — Verify"; at least 10 of first 20 to social-equity; allocation ≤50% retail / ≥50% independent, ≥50% of independent to social-equity; social-equity fee "reduced by 75% — i.e. $2,500 instead of $10,000"; social-equity criteria (51% ownership, ADI ≥7, 90th-percentile incarceration) via NCCR 5.055CorrectedNRS 678B.326, 678B.327, 678B.390(2)Cap exception replaces the open Verify: if, on or before 30 June 2022, the Board issued 20 independent lounge licences, it may thereafter exceed 20 only so long as total independent licences never exceed total retail lounge licences issued (678B.326(2)). Whether the 30 June 2022 trigger was met remains Verify — prospective licences came 30 Nov 2022, after that date, so the trigger is doubtful. Fee correction: 678B.390(2) permits a reduction of up to 75% by regulation — $2,500 is the floor, not a guaranteed fee; reduced independent-lounge initial/renewal fees are likewise by regulation. Allocation and 10-of-20 rules confirmed. ADI/incarceration thresholds: Unverified (regulation text not opened; NRS 678B.323 only requires Board regulations setting a minimum ownership percentage).
§4.6–4.7, §4.10Retail lounge: only holder of an operational adult-use retail licence, attached/immediately adjacent, not on airport property, no initial numeric cap, exempt from re-testing 678B.250 distance bars; one person may not hold both a retail-store and an independent-lounge licence; monopoly cap (greater of 1 licence or 10% in counties ≥100,000, lounges excepted); owners ≥5% register; licences/cards nontransferable; relocation only within same local jurisdiction after local approval; location bars (airport, 1,000 ft school, 300 ft community facility, 1,500 ft gaming, front-door measurement, 1-year expiry); conditional licence pending local compliance/inspectionConfirmedNRS 678B.250, 678B.260, 678B.270, 678B.322, 678B.325, 678B.326, 678B.328, 678B.350, 678B.380, 678B.500(2)Keep, with two additions: 678B.328 is not only a "transfer bar" — it also imposes one-of-each (one independent + one retail lounge per person) with a 2-licence combined cap on 100% acquisition transfer. "No timeline for transfer review" and "12 months to become operational" are CCB FAQ/regulation-level claims, not in 678B.320 itself — keep attributed accordingly. Verify flags on the 1,500-ft gaming buffer and airport bar are removed (verbatim in 678B.250).
§4.5"Labs cannot hold any other establishment licence type" via NRS 678B.290CorrectedNRS 678B.290Attribution deleted: 678B.290 sets testing standards, capability, ISO/IEC 17025 accreditation within 1 year, and Board regulation — it contains no prohibition on a lab holding another licence type. Any independence rule must be sourced to a specific regulation, otherwise it stays Unverified. Lab fees themselves are confirmed above.

§7.2–7.3Exactly three NDA registration types (grower, handler, producer/seed); producer must also be grower/handler; apply by 1 July, expire 31 Dec, 30-day surrender notice, each site separately certified; fingerprint/background per 7 CFR §990.6; criminal-history report "within 120 days" vs "10-year history"; child-support statement; harvest notice ~45 days vs minimum 15 / ideal 30 days; crop must not enter commerce until Dept. compliant-THC report; disposal/remediation if over levelCorrectedNRS 557.200, 557.205, 557.210, 557.240, 557.270; NAC 557.102, 557.108, 557.112, 557.125, 557.136, 557.141Types, calendar, surrender, site certification, child-support statement and commerce/disposal rules confirmed. 120-day rule confirmed as current NAC text (NAC 557.102(1)(c), 557.112(2)(c); NRS 557.200(9)–(10)) — the 2021 State Plan's "10-year report" is a different vintage and must not be merged. NAC 557.108 10-year ineligibility for certain drug felonies (pre-20 Dec 2018 pilot exception) also confirmed. Harvest-notice day counts are form practice, not a NAC rule: NAC 557.125 requires notice before harvest/remediation and sampling per 7 CFR §990.3 but states no day count — keep 45/15/30 days only as NDA-form practice until the current harvest-report form is opened. NDA pre-harvest "minimum 30 days" in §9.1 conflicts with §7.3 form figures — all are form/notice practice, not NAC 557.125 text.

Term/termination/disputes/classification change — term 10 years, automatically renewed for successive 10-year periods unless a party gives written notice no later than 120 days before expiry that it wishes to modify terms; termination on 60 days' written notice if, after dispute process and opportunity to cure, tribal law on testing/labelling/tracking/packaging is materially less restrictive than NRS 453A/453D or is not enforced; disputes: notice, meet-and-confer within 30 days, mediation if unresolved within 60 days, binding AAA arbitration if unresolved within 120 days of mediator selection (arbitrator may not award damages or injunctive/equitable relief); termination on 60 days' notice for uncured material violation; if marijuana's Schedule I classification is altered or federal enforcement policy changes, parties must meet and discuss modification, and if no agreement either party may terminate on 60 days' notice.
